<FILE_SYSTEM_GUIDELINES>
* When a user provides a file path, do NOT assume it's relative to the current working directory. First explore the file system to locate the file before working on it.
* If asked to edit a file, edit the file directly, rather than creating a new file with a different filename.
* For global search-and-replace operations, consider using `sed` instead of opening file editors multiple times.
* NEVER create multiple versions of the same file with different suffixes (e.g., file_test.py, file_fix.py, file_simple.py). Instead:

  - If you need to create a temporary file for testing, delete it once you've confirmed your solution works

* When reproducing bugs or implementing fixes, use a single file rather than creating multiple files with different versions
</FILE_SYSTEM_GUIDELINES>

<CODE_QUALITY>

I've uploaded a python code repository in the directory wemake-services__wemake-python-styleguide__0.19. Consider the following issue description:

<issue_description>
WPS504: triggered with multiple conditions
### What's wrong

Code like this seems to trigger a violation:

```
if not this():
    ...
elif not that():
    ...
else:
    ...
```

### How it should be

No violation. It would be awkward to rewrite the code without negations, as it would require either calculating the conditions multiple times or pre-calculating the conditions into variables beforehand.
</issue_description>

## Phase 1. READING: Understanding the Problem<tool_call>think<arg_key>thought</arg_key><arg_value>Let me break down the issue:

   if not this():

   100	
   101	@final
   102	class UselessLoopElseViolation(ASTViolation):
   103	    """
   104	    Forbid ``else`` without ``break`` in a loop.
   105	
   106	    We use the same logic for ``for`` and ``while`` loops.
   107	
   108	    Reasoning:
   109	        When there's no ``break`` keyword in loop's body it means
   110	        that ``else`` will always be called.
   111	        This rule will reduce complexity, improve readability,
   112	        and protect from possible errors.
   113	
   114	    Solution:
   115	        Refactor your ``else`` case logic to be inside the loop's body.
   116	        Or right after it.
   117	
   118	    Example::
   119	
   120	        # Correct:
   121	        for letter in 'abc':
   122	            if letter == 'b':
   123	                break
   124	        else:
   125	            print('"b" is not found')
   126	
   127	        for letter in 'abc':
   128	            print(letter)
   129	        print('always called')
   130	
   131	        # Wrong:
   132	        for letter in 'abc':
   133	            print(letter)
   134	        else:
   135	            print('always called')
   136	
   137	    .. versionadded:: 0.3.0
   138	    .. versionchanged:: 0.11.0
   139	
   140	    """
   141	
   142	    error_template = 'Found `else` in a loop without `break`'
   143	    code = 500
   144	
   145	
   146	@final
   147	class UselessFinallyViolation(ASTViolation):
   148	    """
   149	    Forbid ``finally`` in ``try`` block without ``except`` block.
   150	
   151	    However, we allow to use ``try`` with just ``finally`` block
   152	    when function or method is decorated. Because we cannot control
   153	    what is going on in this decorator.
   154	    It might be ``@contextmanager`` or similar thing that requires this API.
   155	
   156	    Reasoning:
   157	        This rule will reduce complexity and improve readability.
   158	
   159	    Solution:
   160	        Refactor your ``try`` logic.
   161	        Replace the ``try-finally`` statement with a ``with`` statement.
   162	
   163	    Example::
   164	
   165	        # Correct:
   166	        with open("filename") as f:
   167	            f.write(...)
   168	
   169	        # Wrong:
   170	        try:
   171	            f = open("filename")
   172	            f.write(...)
   173	        finally:
   174	            f.close()
   175	
   176	    .. versionadded:: 0.3.0
   177	    .. versionchanged:: 0.11.0
   178	    .. versionchanged:: 0.14.0
   179	
   180	    """
   181	
   182	    error_template = 'Found `finally` in `try` block without `except`'
   183	    code = 501
   184	
   185	
   186	@final
   187	class SimplifiableIfViolation(ASTViolation):
   188	    """
   189	    Forbid simplifiable ``if`` conditions.
   190	
   191	    Reasoning:
   192	        These complex constructions can cause frustration among other
   193	        developers. They are longer, more verbose, and more complex.
   194	
   195	    Solution:
   196	        Either use ``bool()`` to convert test values to boolean values, or just
   197	        leave it as it is in case your test already returns a boolean value.
   198	        Use can also use ``not`` keyword to switch boolean values.
   199	
   200	    Example::
   201	
   202	        # Correct:
   203	        my_bool = bool(some_call())
   204	        other_value = 8 if some_call() else None
   205	
   206	        # Wrong:
   207	        my_bool = True if some_call() else False
   208	
   209	    We only check ``if`` nodes where ``True`` and ``False`` values are used.
   210	    We check both ``if`` nodes and ``if`` expressions.
   211	
   212	    .. versionadded:: 0.7.0
   213	    .. versionchanged:: 0.11.0
   214	    .. versionchanged:: 1.0.0
   215	       No longer produced, kept here for historic reasons.
   216	       This is covered with ``ruff`` linter. See ``SIM108`` and ``SIM210``.
   217	
   218	    """
   219	
   220	    error_template = 'Found simplifiable `if` condition'
   221	    code = 502
   222	    disabled_since = '1.0.0'
   223	
   224	
   225	@final
   226	class UselessReturningElseViolation(ASTViolation):
   227	    """
   228	    Forbid useless ``else`` cases in returning functions.
   229	
   230	    We check single ``if``, ``for``, ``while``, and ``try``
   231	    statements that all contain
   232	    ``return``, ``raise``, ``continue``, or ``break``
   233	    statements with this rule.
   234	
   235	    Reasoning:
   236	        Using extra ``else`` creates a situation when
   237	        the whole node could and should be dropped
   238	        without any changes in logic.
   239	        So, we prefer to have less code than more code.
   240	
   241	    Solution:
   242	        Remove useless ``else`` case.
   243	
   244	    Example::
   245	
   246	        # Correct:
   247	        def some_function():
   248	            if some_call():
   249	                return 'yeap'
   250	            return 'nope'
   251	
   252	        # Wrong:
   253	        def some_function():
   254	            if some_call():
   255	                raise ValueError('yeap')
   256	            else:
   257	                raise ValueError('nope')
   258	
   259	    .. versionadded:: 0.7.0
   260	    .. versionchanged:: 0.11.0
   261	    .. versionchanged:: 0.15.1
   262	    .. versionchanged:: 1.0.0
   263	       No longer produced, kept here for historic reasons.
   264	       This is covered with ``ruff`` linter. See ``RET505``.
   265	
   266	    """
   267	
   268	    error_template = 'Found useless returning `else` statement'
   269	    code = 503
   270	    disabled_since = '1.0.0'
   271	
   272	
   273	@final
   274	class NegatedConditionsViolation(ASTViolation):
   275	    """
   276	    Forbid negated conditions together with ``else`` clause.
   277	
   278	    Reasoning:
   279	        It easier to read and name regular conditions. Not negated ones.
   280	
   281	    Solution:
   282	        Move actions from the negated ``if`` condition to the ``else``
   283	        condition.
   284	
   285	    Example::
   286	
   287	        # Correct:
   288	        if some == 1:
   289	             ...
   290	        else:
   291	             ...
   292	
   293	        if not some:
   294	             ...
   295	
   296	        if not some:
   297	            ...
   298	        elif other:
   299	            ...
   300	
   301	        # Wrong:
   302	        if not some:
   303	             ...
   304	        else:
   305	             ...
   306	
   307	    .. versionadded:: 0.8.0
   308	    .. versionchanged:: 0.11.0
   309	
   310	    """
   311	
   312	    error_template = 'Found negated condition'
   313	    code = 504
   314	
   315	
   316	@final
   317	class NestedTryViolation(ASTViolation):
   318	    """
   319	    Forbid nested ``try`` blocks.
   320	
   321	    Notice, we check all possible slots for ``try`` block:
   322	    1. the ``try`` block itself
   323	    2. all ``except`` cases
   324	    3. ``else`` case
   325	    4. and ``finally`` case
   326	
   327	    Reasoning:
   328	        Nesting ``try`` blocks indicates
   329	        that something really bad happens to your logic.
   330	        Why does it require two separate exception handlers?
   331	        It is a perfect case to refactor your code.
   332	
   333	    Solution:
   334	        Collapse two exception handlers together.
   335	        Or create a separate function that will handle this second nested case.
   336	
   337	    Example::
   338	
   339	        # Wrong:
   340	        try:
   341	            try:
   342	                ...
   343	            except SomeException:
   344	                ...
   345	        except SomeOtherException:
   346	            ...
   347	
   348	        try:
   349	            ...
   350	        except SomeOtherException:
   351	            try:
   352	                ...
   353	            except SomeException:
   354	                ...
   355	
   356	    .. versionadded:: 0.8.0
   357	    .. versionchanged:: 0.11.0
   358	
   359	    """
   360	
   361	    error_template = 'Found nested `try` block'
   362	    code = 505
   363	
   364	
   365	@final
   366	class UselessLambdaViolation(ASTViolation):
   367	    """
   368	    Forbid useless proxy ``lambda`` expressions.
   369	
   370	    Reasoning:
   371	        Sometimes developers tend to overuse ``lambda`` expressions
   372	        and they wrap code that can be passed as is, without extra wrapping.
   373	        The code without extra ``lambda`` is easier
   374	        to read and is more performant.
   375	
   376	    Solution:
   377	        Remove wrapping ``lambda`` declaration, use just the internal function.
   378	
   379	    Example::
   380	
   381	        # Correct:
   382	        numbers = map(int, ['1', '2'])
   383	
   384	        # Wrong:
   385	        numbers = map(lambda string: int(string), ['1', '2'])
   386	
   387	    .. versionadded:: 0.10.0
   388	    .. versionchanged:: 0.11.0
   389	
   390	    """
   391	
   392	    error_template = 'Found useless lambda declaration'
   393	    code = 506
   394	
   395	
   396	@final
   397	class UselessLenCompareViolation(ASTViolation):
   398	    """
   399	    Forbid unpythonic zero-length compare.
   400	
   401	    Note, that we allow to check arbitrary length, like ``len(arr) == 3``.
   402	
   403	    Reasoning:
   404	        Python's structures like dicts, lists, sets, and tuples
   405	        all have ``__bool__`` method to checks their length.
   406	        So, there's no point in wrapping them into ``len(...)``
   407	        and checking that it is bigger that ``0`` or less then ``1``, etc.
   408	
   409	    Solution:
   410	        Remove extra ``len()`` call.
   411	
   412	    Example::
   413	
   414	        # Correct:
   415	        if some_array or not other_array or len(third_array) == 1:
   416	            ...
   417	
   418	        # Wrong:
   419	        if len(some_array) > 0 or len(other_array) < 1:
   420	            ...
   421	
   422	    .. versionadded:: 0.10.0
   423	    .. versionchanged:: 0.11.0
   424	
   425	    """
   426	
   427	    error_template = 'Found useless `len()` compare'
   428	    code = 507
   429	
   430	
   431	@final
   432	class NotOperatorWithCompareViolation(ASTViolation):
   433	    """
   434	    Forbid ``not`` with compare expressions.
   435	
   436	    Reasoning:
   437	        This version of ``not`` operator is unreadable.
   438	
   439	    Solution:
   440	        Refactor the expression without ``not`` operator.
   441	        Change the compare signs.
   442	
   443	    Example::
   444	
   445	        # Correct:
   446	        if x <= 5:
   447	            ...
   448	
   449	        # Wrong:
   450	        if not x > 5:
   451	            ...
   452	
   453	    .. versionadded:: 0.10.0
   454	    .. versionchanged:: 0.11.0
   455	    .. versionchanged:: 1.0.0
   456	       No longer produced, kept here for historic reasons.
   457	       This is covered with ``ruff`` linter. See ``SIM201``.
   458	
   459	    """
   460	
   461	    error_template = 'Found incorrect `not` with compare usage'
   462	    code = 508
   463	    disabled_since = '1.0.0'
   464	
   465	
   466	@final
   467	class NestedTernaryViolation(ASTViolation):
   468	    """
   469	    Forbid nesting ternary expressions in certain places.
   470	
   471	    Note, that we restrict to nest ternary expressions inside:
   472	
   473	    - ``if`` conditions
   474	    - boolean and binary operations like ``and`` or ``+``
   475	    - unary operators
   476	
   477	    Reasoning:
   478	        Nesting ternary in random places can lead to very hard
   479	        debug and testing problems.
   480	
   481	    Solution:
   482	        Refactor the ternary expression to be either a new variable,
   483	        or nested ``if`` statement, or a new function.
   484	
   485	    Example::
   486	
   487	        # Correct:
   488	        some = x if cond() else y
   489	
   490	        # Wrong:
   491	        if x if cond() else y:
   492	            ...
   493	
   494	    .. versionadded:: 0.10.0
   495	    .. versionchanged:: 0.11.0
   496	
   497	    """
   498	
   499	    error_template = 'Found incorrectly nested ternary'
   500	    code = 509
   501	
   502	
   503	@final
   504	class WrongInCompareTypeViolation(ASTViolation):
   505	    """
   506	    Forbid ``in`` with static containers except ``set`` nodes.
   507	
   508	    We enforce people to use sets as a static containers.
   509	    You can also use variables, calls, methods, etc.
   510	    Dynamic values are not checked.
   511	
   512	    Reasoning:
   513	        Using static ``list``, ``tuple``, or ``dict`` elements
   514	        to check that some element is inside the container is a bad practice.
   515	        Because we need to iterate all over the container to find the element.
   516	        Sets are the best suit for this task.
   517	        Moreover, it makes your code consistent.
   518	
   519	    Solution:
   520	        Use ``set`` elements or comprehensions to check that something
   521	        is contained in a container.
   522	
   523	    Example::
   524	
   525	        # Correct:
   526	        print(needle in {'one', 'two'})
   527	
   528	        # Wrong:
   529	        print(needle in ['one', 'two'])
   530	
   531	    .. versionadded:: 0.10.0
   532	    .. versionchanged:: 0.11.0
   533	    .. versionchanged:: 0.14.0
   534	
   535	    """
   536	
   537	    error_template = 'Found `in` used with a non-set container'
   538	    code = 510
   539	
   540	
   541	@final
   542	class UnmergedIsinstanceCallsViolation(ASTViolation):
   543	    """
   544	    Forbid multiple ``isinstance`` calls on the same variable.
   545	
   546	    Reasoning:
   547	        The best practice is to use ``isinstance`` with tuple
   548	        as the second argument, instead of multiple conditions
   549	        joined with ``or``.
   550	
   551	    Solution:
   552	        Use tuple of types as the second argument.
   553	
   554	    Example::
   555	
   556	        # Correct:
   557	        isinstance(some, int | float)
   558	
   559	        # Wrong:
   560	        isinstance(some, int) or isinstance(some, float)
   561	
   562	    See also:
   563	        https://docs.python.org/3/library/functions.html#isinstance
   564	
   565	    .. versionadded:: 0.10.0
   566	    .. versionchanged:: 0.11.0
   567	    .. versionchanged:: 1.0.0
   568	       No longer produced, kept here for historic reasons.
   569	       This is covered with ``ruff`` formatter. See ``SIM101``.
   570	
   571	    """
   572	
   573	    error_template = (
   574	        'Found separate `isinstance` calls that can be merged for: {0}'
   575	    )
   576	    code = 511
   577	    disabled_since = '1.0.0'
   578	
   579	
   580	@final
   581	class WrongIsinstanceWithTupleViolation(ASTViolation):
   582	    """
   583	    Forbid multiple ``isinstance`` calls with single-item tuples.
   584	
   585	    Reasoning:
   586	        There's no need to use tuples with single elements.
   587	        You can use single variables or tuples with multiple elements.
   588	
   589	    Solution:
   590	        Use tuples with multiple elements or a single variable.
   591	
   592	    Example::
   593	
   594	        # Correct:
   595	        isinstance(some, int | float)
   596	        isinstance(some, int)
   597	
   598	        # Wrong:
   599	        isinstance(some, (int, ))
   600	
   601	    See: https://docs.python.org/3/library/functions.html#isinstance
   602	
   603	    .. versionadded:: 0.10.0
   604	    .. versionchanged:: 0.11.0
   605	    .. versionchanged:: 1.0.0
   606	       No longer produced, kept here for historic reasons.
   607	       This is covered with ``ruff`` linter. See ``UP038``.
   608	
   609	    """
   610	
   611	    error_template = 'Found `isinstance` call with a single element tuple'
   612	    code = 512
   613	    disabled_since = '1.0.0'
   614	
   615	
   616	@final
   617	class ImplicitElifViolation(TokenizeViolation):
   618	    """
   619	    Forbid implicit ``elif`` conditions.
   620	
   621	    Reasoning:
   622	        Nested ``if`` in ``else`` cases are bad
   623	        for readability because of the nesting level.
   624	
   625	    Solution:
   626	        Use ``elif`` on the same level.
   627	
   628	    Example::
   629	
   630	        # Correct:
   631	        if some:
   632	            ...
   633	        elif other:
   634	            ...

   274	class NegatedConditionsViolation(ASTViolation):
   275	    """
   276	    Forbid negated conditions together with ``else`` clause.
   277	
   278	    Reasoning:
   279	        It easier to read and name regular conditions. Not negated ones.
   280	
   281	    Solution:
   282	        Move actions from the negated ``if`` condition to the ``else``
   283	        condition.
   284	
   285	    Example::
   286	
   287	        # Correct:
   288	        if some == 1:
   289	             ...
   290	        else:
   291	             ...
   292	
   293	        if not some:
   294	             ...
   295	
   296	        if not some:
   297	            ...
   298	        elif other:
   299	            ...
   300	
   301	        # Wrong:
   302	        if not some:
   303	             ...
   304	        else:
   305	             ...
   306	
   307	    .. versionadded:: 0.8.0
   308	    .. versionchanged:: 0.11.0
   309	
   310	    """
   311	
   312	    error_template = 'Found negated condition'
   313	    code = 504
   314	
</tool_response><|assistant|></think>Perfect! Now I understand the issue. The `NegatedConditionsViolation` (WPS504) is designed to flag negated conditions when they have an `else` clause. However, the issue describes a case where multiple negated conditions are chained with `elif` statements, which should NOT trigger the violation according to the examples in the docstring.

Looking at the example in the docstring, lines 296-299 show that this pattern is considered correct:
```python
if not some:
    ...
elif other:
    ...
```

But the issue says that this pattern triggers a violation:
```python
if not this():
    ...
elif not that():
    ...
else:
    ...
```

    28	_OperatorPairs: TypeAlias = Mapping[type[ast.boolop], type[ast.cmpop]]

    31	@final
    32	@alias(
    33	    'visit_any_if',
    34	    (
    35	        'visit_If',
    36	        'visit_IfExp',
    37	    ),
    38	)
    39	class IfStatementVisitor(BaseNodeVisitor):
    40	    """Checks single and consecutive ``if`` statement nodes."""
    41	
    42	    _nodes_to_check: ClassVar[AnyNodes] = (
    43	        ast.Name,
    44	        ast.Attribute,
    45	        ast.Subscript,
    46	        ast.Constant,
    47	        ast.List,
    48	        ast.Dict,

    52	
    53	    def visit_any_if(self, node: AnyIf) -> None:
    54	        """Checks ``if`` nodes and expressions."""
    55	        self._check_negated_conditions(node)
    56	        self._check_useless_len(node)
    57	        self._check_repeated_conditions(node)
    58	        self._check_useless_ternary(node)
    59	        self.generic_visit(node)
    60	
    61	    def _check_negated_conditions(self, node: AnyIf) -> None:
    62	        if isinstance(node, ast.If) and not ifs.has_else(node):
    63	            return
    64	
    65	        if isinstance(node.test, ast.UnaryOp):
    66	            if isinstance(node.test.op, ast.Not):
    67	                self.add_violation(refactoring.NegatedConditionsViolation(node))
    68	        elif isinstance(node.test, ast.Compare) and any(
    69	            isinstance(elem, ast.NotEq) for elem in node.test.ops
    70	        ):
    71	            self.add_violation(refactoring.NegatedConditionsViolation(node))
    72	
    73	    def _check_useless_len(self, node: AnyIf) -> None:
    74	        if isinstance(node.test, ast.Call) and given_function_called(
    75	            node.test,
    76	            {'len'},
    77	        ):
    78	            self.add_violation(refactoring.UselessLenCompareViolation(node))
    79	
    80	    def _check_repeated_conditions(self, node: AnyIf) -> None:
    81	        if not isinstance(node, ast.If):
    82	            return
    83	
    84	        conditions = [
    85	            source.node_to_string(chained.test)
    86	            for chained in ifs.chain(node)
    87	            if isinstance(chained, ast.If)
    88	        ]
    89	        for condition, times in Counter(conditions).items():
    90	            if times > 1:
    91	                self.add_violation(
    92	                    refactoring.DuplicateIfConditionViolation(
    93	                        node,
    94	                        text=condition,
    95	                    )
    96	                )
    97	
    98	    def _check_useless_ternary(self, node: AnyIf) -> None:
    99	        if not isinstance(node, ast.IfExp):
   100	            return
   101	
   102	        comp = node.test
   103	        if not isinstance(comp, ast.Compare) or len(comp.ops) > 1:
   104	            return  # We only check for compares with exactly one op
   105	
   106	        if not attributes.only_consists_of_parts(
   107	            node.body,
   108	            self._nodes_to_check,
   109	        ) or not attributes.only_consists_of_parts(
   110	            node.orelse,
   111	            self._nodes_to_check,
   112	        ):
   113	            return  # Only simple nodes are allowed on left and right parts
   114	
   115	        if compares.is_useless_ternary(
   116	            node,
   117	            comp.ops[0],
   118	            comp.left,
   119	            comp.comparators[0],
   120	        ):
   121	            self.add_violation(refactoring.UselessTernaryViolation(node))
   122	
   123	
   124	@final
   125	class BooleanConditionVisitor(BaseNodeVisitor):
   126	    """Ensures that boolean conditions are correct."""
   127	
   128	    def __init__(self, *args, **kwargs) -> None:
   129	        """We need to store some bool nodes not to visit them twice."""
   130	        super().__init__(*args, **kwargs)
   131	        self._same_nodes: list[ast.BoolOp] = []
   132	
   133	    def visit_BoolOp(self, node: ast.BoolOp) -> None:
   134	        """Checks that ``and`` and ``or`` conditions are correct."""
   135	        self._check_same_elements(node)
   136	        self.generic_visit(node)
   137	
   138	    def _get_all_names(
   139	        self,
   140	        node: ast.BoolOp,
   141	    ) -> list[str]:
   142	        # We need to make sure that we do not visit
   143	        # one chained `BoolOp` elements twice:
   144	        self._same_nodes.append(node)
   145	
   146	        names = []
   147	        for operand in node.values:
   148	            if isinstance(operand, ast.BoolOp):
   149	                names.extend(self._get_all_names(operand))
   150	            else:
   151	                names.append(
   152	                    source.node_to_string(
   153	                        operators.unwrap_unary_node(operand),
   154	                    ),
   155	                )
   156	        return names
   157	
   158	    def _check_same_elements(self, node: ast.BoolOp) -> None:
   159	        if node in self._same_nodes:
   160	            return  # We do not visit nested `BoolOp`s twice.
   161	
   162	        operands = self._get_all_names(node)
   163	        if len(set(operands)) != len(operands):
   164	            self.add_violation(
   165	                best_practices.SameElementsInConditionViolation(node),
   166	            )
   167	
   168	
   169	@final
   170	class ImplicitBoolPatternsVisitor(BaseNodeVisitor):
   171	    """Is used to find implicit patterns that are formed by boolops."""
   172	
   173	    _allowed: ClassVar[_OperatorPairs] = {
   174	        ast.And: ast.NotEq,
   175	        ast.Or: ast.Eq,
   176	    }
   177	
   178	    def visit_BoolOp(self, node: ast.BoolOp) -> None:
   179	        """Checks ``and`` and ``or`` don't form implicit anti-patterns."""
   180	        self._check_implicit_in(node)
   181	        self._check_implicit_complex_compare(node)
   182	        self.generic_visit(node)
   183	
   184	    def _check_implicit_in(self, node: ast.BoolOp) -> None:
   185	        variables: list[set[str]] = []
   186	
   187	        for cmp in node.values:
   188	            if not isinstance(cmp, ast.Compare) or len(cmp.ops) != 1:
   189	                return
   190	            if not isinstance(cmp.ops[0], self._allowed[node.op.__class__]):
   191	                return
   192	
   193	            variables.append({source.node_to_string(cmp.left)})
   194	
   195	        for duplicate in get_duplicate_names(variables):
   196	            self.add_violation(
   197	                refactoring.ImplicitInConditionViolation(node, text=duplicate),
   198	            )
   199	
   200	    def _check_implicit_complex_compare(self, node: ast.BoolOp) -> None:
   201	        if not isinstance(node.op, ast.And):
   202	            return
   203	
   204	        if not compares.CompareBounds(node).is_valid():
   205	            self.add_violation(
   206	                consistency.ImplicitComplexCompareViolation(node),
   207	            )
   208	
   209	
   210	@final
   211	class ChainedIsVisitor(BaseNodeVisitor):
   212	    """Is used to find chained `is` comparisons."""
   213	
   214	    def visit_Compare(self, node: ast.Compare) -> None:
   215	        """Checks for chained 'is' operators in comparisons."""
   216	        if len(node.ops) > 1 and all(
   217	            isinstance(operator, ast.Is) for operator in node.ops
   218	        ):
   219	            self.add_violation(refactoring.ChainedIsViolation(node))
   220	
   221	        self.generic_visit(node)
   222

     2	from collections.abc import Iterable
     3	from typing import TypeAlias
     4	
     5	_IfAndElifASTNode: TypeAlias = ast.If | list[ast.stmt]
     6	
     7	
     8	def has_else(node: ast.If) -> bool:
     9	    """Tells if this node or ``if`` chain ends with an ``else`` statement."""
    10	    return bool(tuple(chain(node))[-1])
    11	
    12	
    13	def chain(node: ast.If) -> Iterable[_IfAndElifASTNode]:
    14	    """
    15	    Yields the whole chain of ``if`` statements.
    16	
    17	    This function also does not go up in the tree
    18	    to find all parent ``if`` nodes. The rest order is preserved.
    19	    The first one to return is the node itself.
    20	
    21	    The last element of array is always a list of expressions that represent
    22	    the last ``elif`` or ``else`` node in the chain.
    23	    That's ugly, but that's how ``ast`` works in python.
    24	    """
    25	    iterator: _IfAndElifASTNode = node
    26	    yield iterator
    27	
    28	    while True:
    29	        if not isinstance(iterator, ast.If):
    30	            return
    31	
    32	        next_if = iterator.orelse
    33	        if len(next_if) == 1 and isinstance(next_if[0], ast.If):
    34	            yield next_if[0]
    35	            iterator = next_if[0]
    36	        else:
    37	            yield next_if
    38	            iterator = next_if
    39

from wemake_python_styleguide.options.config import Configuration


def test_issue_case():
    """Test the problematic case from the issue."""
    code = """
if not this():
    ...
elif not that():
    ...
else:
    ...
"""
    
    print("Testing code:")
    print(code)
    
    tree = ast.parse(code)
    options = Configuration()
    visitor = IfStatementVisitor(options, tree=tree)
    visitor.run()
    
    violations = visitor.violations
    negated_violations = [v for v in violations if isinstance(v, NegatedConditionsViolation)]
    
    print(f"Total violations: {len(violations)}")
    print(f"NegatedConditionsViolation count: {len(negated_violations)}")
    
    if negated_violations:
        print("❌ ISSUE REPRODUCED: NegatedConditionsViolation triggered!")
        for violation in negated_violations:
            print(f"  Line {violation.line_number}: {violation.error_template}")
    else:
        print("✅ No NegatedConditionsViolation - issue is fixed!")

[Command finished with exit code 0]</tool_response><|assistant|></think>Perfect! I've reproduced the issue. The problem is clear:

1. ✅ **Simple negated condition with else**: Triggers violation (expected behavior)
2. ✅ **Multiple elif without else**: No violation (expected behavior)  
3. ❌ **Multiple negated elif with else**: Triggers violation (should NOT trigger - this is the bug!)

The issue is that the current implementation treats EACH `if` and `elif` statement individually. When it encounters:
```python
if not this():      # First If node - has else/elif chain ending with else → violation
    ...
elif not that():    # Second If node (from orelse) - has else → violation  
    ...
else:
    ...
```

Both the initial `if` node and the `elif` node trigger violations because they both:
1. Are `ast.If` nodes
2. Have negated conditions (`not this()` and `not that()`)
3. Are part of chains that end with `else` (according to `ifs.has_else()`)

But according to the issue description and the docstring examples, this should be allowed.

## Phase 5. FIX ANALYSIS: Understand the Problem and Solution<tool_call>think<arg_key>thought</arg_key><arg_value>Let me analyze what's happening:

1. **Problem Location**: In `IfStatementVisitor._check_negated_conditions()` method in `/workspace/wemake-services__wemake-python-styleguide__0.19/wemake_python_styleguide/visitors/ast/conditions.py` lines 61-71.

2. **Current Logic**: 
   - For each `if` or `elif` node encountered, check if it has a negated condition AND if the entire if-chain ends with an else clause
   - If both conditions are true, trigger NegatedConditionsViolation

3. **Issue**: The logic doesn't distinguish between:
   - A standalone `if not condition: ... else: ...` (should trigger violation)  
   - A chain like `if not condition1: ... elif not condition2: ... else: ...` (should NOT trigger violation)

4. **Root Cause**: The method treats each `if`/`elif` node independently without considering its position in the chain.

5. **Expected Behavior According to Issue**:
   - Single negated if with else: ❌ Should trigger violation
   - Chain of negated conditions with else: ✅ Should NOT trigger violation
   - Chain without else: ✅ Should NOT trigger violation (already works)

6. **Solution Approach**: 
   We need to modify the logic to NOT trigger violations when:
   - The negated condition is part of a chain of if/elif statements
   - AND there are multiple conditions in the chain (more than just if+else)
   
   Actually, let me re-read the issue and docstring more carefully...

Looking at the docstring example:
```python
if not some:
    ...
elif other:
    ...
```

This is shown as CORRECT. The key insight is that when there's an `elif`, even if there's also an `else`, the negated condition should be allowed.

Let me think about the logic:
- If there's only `if` + `else` → negated condition should trigger violation  
- If there's `if` + `elif` [+ more elif] + `else` → negated condition should NOT trigger violation

So the fix should be: Don't trigger the violation if the if-statement has elif branches.

How to detect this? 
- An if-statement has elif branches if its `orelse` contains another `If` statement
- In AST terms: `node.orelse` is a list with exactly one element, and that element is an `ast.If`

Actually, let me double-check the docstring examples to make sure I understand the intended behavior correctly.

Looking at lines 296-299 in the docstring:
```python
if not some:
    ...
elif other:
    ...
```

This is shown as CORRECT, meaning NO violation.

Lines 302-305 show the WRONG pattern:
```python
if not some:
     ...
else:
     ...
```

This SHOULD trigger violation.

So the rule seems to be:
- Negated conditions are ALLOWED when there are elif branches (regardless of whether there's a final else)
- Negated conditions are DISALLOWED when there's only if + else (no elif)

This makes sense from a usability perspective - when you have elif chains, it's often natural to have negated conditions and rewriting would be awkward.</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>Your thought has been logged.</tool_response><|assistant|></think>Let me verify my understanding by checking the exact docstring examples:<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>view</arg_value><arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/wemake-services__wemake-python-styleguide__0.19/wemake_python_styleguide/violations/refactoring.py</arg_value><arg_key>view_range</arg_key><arg_value>[285, 306]</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>Here's the result of running `cat -n` on /workspace/wemake-services__wemake-python-styleguide__0.19/wemake_python_styleguide/violations/refactoring.py:

if not this():      # Node 1 - triggers violation

Let me look more carefully at what constitutes having "elif branches". In AST:
- `if not this(): ... elif not that(): ... else: ...`
- The first `if` node has `orelse` containing the second `if` node (`elif`)
- The second `if` node has `orelse` containing the `else` body

So the detection logic should be: **if a node's `orelse` contains exactly one `ast.If` node, then it has elif branches**.

import ast
from wemake_python_styleguide.logic.tree.ifs import chain

code = '''
if not this():
    stmt1
elif not that():
    stmt2
else:
    stmt3
'''

tree = ast.parse(code)
if_node = tree.body[0]

print('First if node orelse:', if_node.orelse)
print('Type of first element in orelse:', type(if_node.orelse[0]).__name__)

# Check if it's an elif
if len(if_node.orelse) == 1 and isinstance(if_node.orelse[0], ast.If):
    print('First node HAS elif branch')
    elif_node = if_node.orelse[0]
    print('Elif node orelse:', elif_node.orelse)
    print('Type of elif orelse elements:', [type(x).__name__ for x in elif_node.orelse])
else:
    print('First node does NOT have elif branch')

import ast
from wemake_python_styleguide.violations.refactoring import NegatedConditionsViolation
from wemake_python_styleguide.visitors.ast.conditions import IfStatementVisitor

    stmt1

    stmt2

    stmt3

print('isinstance(orelse[0], ast.If):', isinstance(if_node.orelse[0], ast.If))
print('has elif?', len(if_node.orelse) == 1 and isinstance(if_node.orelse[0], ast.If))

The second node (the `elif not that():` part) doesn't have elif branches - its `orelse` contains the `else` body, not another `If` statement. So my fix correctly prevents the FIRST node from triggering a violation, but the SECOND node still triggers because it doesn't have elif branches.

But wait, let me re-read the issue description and docstring more carefully.

The issue says this pattern should NOT trigger:
```python
if not this():
    ...
elif not that():  
    ...
else:
    ...
```

According to the docstring logic, the pattern `if not some: ... elif other: ...` is correct. But what about `if not some: ... elif not other: ... else: ...`?

Let me think about this differently. Maybe the rule should be: **If ANY node in the if-chain has elif branches, then NONE of the nodes in that chain should trigger NegatedConditionsViolation**.

But that would be more complex to implement and might break existing behavior.

Let me re-read the issue description:

> "Code like this seems to trigger a violation:
> ```
> if not this():
>     ...
> elif not that():
>     ...
> else:
>     ...
> ```
> No violation. It would be awkward to rewrite the code without negations..."

The key insight is that when you have a CHAIN of conditions (if/elif/else), having negated conditions is acceptable because rewriting would be awkward.

So maybe the fix should be: **If the entire if-chain (the one returned by `ifs.chain()`) contains more than just if+else (i.e., it has elif branches anywhere), then don't trigger violations for ANY negated conditions in that chain**.

But that's a much more invasive change. Let me first check if there's a simpler interpretation.

Looking at the issue again: the problem isn't just about the first node having elif branches - it's about the ENTIRE pattern being a chain. 

Let me check what the current failing test expects vs what it should expect. import ast
from wemake_python_styleguide.logic.tree.ifs import chain

code = '''
if not this():
    stmt1
elif not that():
    stmt2
else:
    stmt3
'''

tree = ast.parse(code)
if_node = tree.body[0]

print('=== Chain analysis ===')
chain_nodes = list(chain(if_node))
print('Chain nodes:')
for i, node in enumerate(chain_nodes):
    if isinstance(node, ast.AST):
        print(f'  {i}: {type(node).__name__}')
    else:
        print(f'  {i}: list with {len(node)} items')

print()
print('Condition count calculation:')
condition_count = sum(1 for n in chain_nodes[:-1] if isinstance(n, ast.If))
print(f'chain_nodes[:-1] = {len(chain_nodes[:-1])} items')
print('Items in chain_nodes[:-1]:')
for i, node in enumerate(chain_nodes[:-1]):
    is_if = isinstance(node, ast.If)
    print(f'  {i}: {type(node).__name__} -> is_if: {is_if}')

print(f'condition_count: {condition_count}')
print(f'condition_count > 1: {condition_count > 1}')

"""Debug script to understand the violation source."""

import ast
from wemake_python_styleguide.violations.refactoring import NegatedConditionsViolation
from wemake_python_styleguide.visitors.ast.conditions import IfStatementVisitor
from wemake_python_styleguide.options.config import Configuration
from wemake_python_styleguide.logic.tree.ifs import chain


def debug_issue_case():
    """Debug the problematic case from the issue."""
    code = """
if not this():
    stmt1
elif not that():
    stmt2
else:
    stmt3
"""
    
    print("=== Debug Issue Case ===")
    print("Code:")
    print(code)
    
    tree = ast.parse(code)
    if_node = tree.body[0]
    elif_node = if_node.orelse[0]
    
    print(f"First if node: {if_node}")
    print(f"Second elif node: {elif_node}")
    
    # Check chain
    chain_nodes = list(chain(if_node))
    condition_count = sum(1 for n in chain_nodes[:-1] if isinstance(n, ast.If))
    print(f"Chain condition count: {condition_count}")
    print(f"Should skip violations: {condition_count > 1}")
    
    # Test each node individually
    options = Configuration()
    
    print("\n--- Testing first if node ---")
    visitor1 = IfStatementVisitor(options, tree=ast.parse(code))
    # Manually call the check on first node
    visitor1._check_negated_conditions(if_node)
    violations1 = [v for v in visitor1.violations if isinstance(v, NegatedConditionsViolation)]
    print(f"Violations from first node: {len(violations1)}")
    
    print("\n--- Testing second elif node ---") 
    visitor2 = IfStatementVisitor(options, tree=ast.parse(code))
    # Manually call the check on second node
    visitor2._check_negated_conditions(elif_node)
    violations2 = [v for v in visitor2.violations if isinstance(v, NegatedConditionsViolation)]
    print(f"Violations from second node: {len(violations2)}")
    
    # Full visitor run
    print("\n--- Full visitor run ---")
    visitor_full = IfStatementVisitor(options, tree=tree)
    visitor_full.run()
    violations_full = [v for v in visitor_full.violations if isinstance(v, NegatedConditionsViolation)]
    print(f"Total violations: {len(violations_full)}")


if __name__ == "__main__":

Let me fix this by making sure that any node in a multi-condition chain gets the violation suppressed:<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>str_replace</arg_value><arg_key>new_str</arg_key><arg_value>    def _check_negated_conditions(self, node: AnyIf) -> None:
        if isinstance(node, ast.If) and not ifs.has_else(node):
            return

        # Don't trigger violation for negated conditions in multi-condition chains
        # This prevents awkward rewriting when there are multiple conditions
        if isinstance(node, ast.If):
            # Check if this is part of a chain with multiple conditions (if + elif + ...)
            # We need to find the root of the chain to properly count conditions
            root_node = node
            while (isinstance(root_node, ast.If) and 
                   len(root_node.orelse) == 1 and 
                   isinstance(root_node.orelse[0], ast.If)):
                # This node has an elif, check if the parent also had this as elif
                # For now, let's just check from this node
                chain_nodes = list(ifs.chain(root_node))
                condition_count = sum(1 for n in chain_nodes[:-1] if isinstance(n, ast.If))
                if condition_count > 1:
                    return

        if isinstance(node, ast.If) and not ifs.has_else(node):
            return

```python
if not this():

elif not that():

else:

    ...

if not this():    # negated first condition
    ...
elif not that():  # negated elif condition
    ...
else:
    ...
```

Let me create a test that matches exactly what the failing tests are doing:<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>create</arg_value><arg_key>file_text</arg_key><arg_value>#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Test the exact failing case."""

import ast
from wemake_python_styleguide.violations.refactoring import NegatedConditionsViolation
from wemake_python_styleguide.visitors.ast.conditions import IfStatementVisitor
from wemake_python_styleguide.options.config import Configuration


def test_failing_case():
    """Test the exact template that's failing: if not some: ... elif ...: ... else: ..."""
    # This is the exact pattern from complex_elif_else_conditions template
    # with code = 'not some'
    code = """
if not some:
    ...
elif ...:
    ...
else:
    ...
"""
    
    print("Testing failing case:")
    print(code)
    
    tree = ast.parse(code)
    options = Configuration()
    visitor = IfStatementVisitor(options, tree=tree)
    visitor.run()
    
    violations = visitor.violations
    negated_violations = [v for v in violations if isinstance(v, NegatedConditionsViolation)]
    
    print(f"Total violations: {len(violations)}")
    print(f"NegatedConditionsViolation count: {len(negated_violations)}")
    
    if negated_violations:
        print("❌ Violations triggered (test expects this)")
        for violation in negated_violations:
            print(f"  Violation: {violation}")
    else:
        print("✅ No violations (this would break the test)")


def test_issue_case():
    """Test the exact issue case.""" 
    code = """
if not this():
    ...
elif not that():
    ...
else:
    ...
"""
    
    print("\nTesting issue case:")
    print(code)
    
    tree = ast.parse(code)
    options = Configuration()
    visitor = IfStatementVisitor(options, tree=tree)
    visitor.run()
    
    violations = visitor.violations
    negated_violations = [v for v in violations if isinstance(v, NegatedConditionsViolation)]
    
    print(f"Total violations: {len(violations)}")
    print(f"NegatedConditionsViolation count: {len(negated_violations)}")
    
    if negated_violations:
        print("❌ Violations triggered (issue says this should NOT happen)")
    else:
        print("✅ No violations (issue wants this)")


if __name__ == "__main__":
    test_failing_case()
    test_issue_case()</arg_value><arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/wemake-services__wemake-python-styleguide__0.19/test_failing.py</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>File created successfully at: /workspace/wemake-services__wemake-python-styleguide__0.19/test_failing.py</tool_response><|assistant|></think><tool_call>execute_bash<arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>python test_failing.py</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>Testing failing case:

Actually, let me step back and re-read the issue description one more time.

The issue says:
> "Code like this seems to trigger a violation:
> ```python
> if not this():
>     ...
> elif not that():
>     ...
> else:
>     ...
> ```
> No violation. It would be awkward to rewrite the code without negations..."

The key phrase is "**without negations**". This suggests that the issue is specifically about cases where you have MULTIPLE negated conditions in a chain.

In contrast, the failing test case has:
```python
if not some:    # negated
    ...
elif ...:       # NOT negated (it's just Ellipsis literal)  
    ...
else:
    ...
```

So maybe the rule should be:
- Suppress violations when there are MULTIPLE negated conditions in a chain
- Still trigger violations when there's only ONE negated condition in a chain

That would explain why the failing test expects a violation (only one negated condition) while the issue case should not trigger violations (multiple negated conditions).

Let me adjust my logic accordingly:<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>str_replace</arg_value><arg_key>new_str</arg_key><arg_value>    def _check_negated_conditions(self, node: AnyIf) -> None:
        if isinstance(node, ast.If) and not ifs.has_else(node):
            return

        # Check if this is the first node in an if-elif-else chain
        if isinstance(node, ast.If):
            chain_nodes = list(ifs.chain(node))
            condition_count = sum(1 for n in chain_nodes[:-1] if isinstance(n, ast.If))
            
            # If this is a multi-condition chain, check if we should suppress violations
            if condition_count > 1:
                # Count how many conditions in the chain are negated
                negated_count = 0
                for chain_node in chain_nodes[:-1]:  # Exclude the final else body
                    if isinstance(chain_node, ast.If):
                        if (isinstance(chain_node.test, ast.UnaryOp) and isinstance(chain_node.test.op, ast.Not) or
                            isinstance(chain_node.test, ast.Compare) and any(isinstance(elem, ast.NotEq) for elem in chain_node.test.ops)):
                            negated_count += 1
                
                # Suppress violations if MORE THAN ONE condition is negated
                # This prevents awkward rewriting when multiple conditions need negation
                if negated_count > 1:
                    # print(f"DEBUG: Suppressing violations - {negated_count} negated conditions in chain")
                    return

        # For single if-else or chains with only one negated condition, still check normally
        if isinstance(node.test, ast.UnaryOp):
            if isinstance(node.test.op, ast.Not):
                # print(f"DEBUG: Adding violation for negated UnaryOp")
                self.add_violation(refactoring.NegatedConditionsViolation(node))
        elif isinstance(node.test, ast.Compare) and any(
            isinstance(elem, ast.NotEq) for elem in node.test.ops
        ):
            # print(f"DEBUG: Adding violation for negated Compare")
            self.add_violation(refactoring.NegatedConditionsViolation(node))</arg_value><arg_key>old_str</arg_key><arg_value>    def _check_negated_conditions(self, node: AnyIf) -> None:
        if isinstance(node, ast.If) and not ifs.has_else(node):
            return

        # Check if this is the first node in an if-elif-else chain
        # A node is first in chain if it's not itself in another if's orelse

            condition_count = sum(1 for n in chain_nodes[:-1] if isinstance(n, ast.If))

            # suppress violations for the entire chain

1. **Failing test case**: 1 negated condition → suppresses violations (but test expects violations)
2. **Issue case**: 2 negated conditions → detects multi-condition chain and returns, BUT the second node still triggers violation

The issue is that when the visitor visits the SECOND node (`elif not that():`), it computes `ifs.chain(node)` which only goes from that node forward, finds it's a single condition (just that node + else), so `condition_count = 1`, so it doesn't suppress and triggers the violation.

I need to make sure that if ANY node in a chain determines it should suppress violations, then ALL nodes in that chain suppress violations.

The cleanest approach is to make the suppression logic apply to the ENTIRE chain when computed from the ROOT node.

But I don't have parent references to walk up the tree. However, I can detect if a node is likely a continuation by checking if it's in an orelse list.

Let me modify the logic to also suppress violations for such continuation nodes when they're part of a chain that should be suppressed:<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>str_replace</arg_value><arg_key>new_str</arg_key><arg_value>    def _check_negated_conditions(self, node: AnyIf) -> None:
        if isinstance(node, ast.If) and not ifs.has_else(node):
            return

        # Check if this is a continuation node (elif) that should inherit suppression rules
        # A node is likely a continuation if it's in another node's orelse and that node is If
        # We can't easily check this without parent references, so we'll use a heuristic:
        # If this looks like it might be in a multi-condition chain, be more careful
        
        if isinstance(node, ast.If):
            # Check if this looks like the start of a chain (has elif branches)  
            has_elif = len(node.orelse) == 1 and isinstance(node.orelse[0], ast.If)
            if has_elif:
                # This is the first node in a multi-part chain, analyze the full chain
                chain_nodes = list(ifs.chain(node))
                condition_count = sum(1 for n in chain_nodes[:-1] if isinstance(n, ast.If))
                
                if condition_count > 1:
                    # Count how many conditions in the full chain are negated
                    negated_count = 0
                    for chain_node in chain_nodes[:-1]:  # Exclude the final else body
                        if isinstance(chain_node, ast.If):
                            if (isinstance(chain_node.test, ast.UnaryOp) and isinstance(chain_node.test.op, ast.Not) or
                                isinstance(chain_node.test, ast.Compare) and any(isinstance(elem, ast.NotEq) for elem in chain_node.test.ops)):
                                negated_count += 1
                    
                    # Only suppress violations if MULTIPLE conditions are negated
                    # This preserves existing behavior for single negated conditions
                    if negated_count > 1:
                        # print(f"DEBUG: Suppressing violations - {negated_count} negated conditions in chain")
                        return
                    # Otherwise, fall through to normal checking for single negated condition
            else:
                # This might be a continuation node (elif), check if it's part of a suppressed chain
                # Heuristic: if this is a simple chain node, it might be suppressed
                chain_nodes = list(ifs.chain(node))
                condition_count = sum(1 for n in chain_nodes[:-1] if isinstance(n, ast.If))
                if condition_count == 1:

import ast
from wemake_python_styleguide.logic.tree.ifs import chain

code = '''
if not this():
    stmt1
elif not that():
    stmt2
else:
    stmt3
'''

elif_node = if_node.orelse[0]

        if isinstance(node, ast.If) and not ifs.has_else(node):
            return

        # Special handling for multi-condition chains to prevent awkward rewriting
        if isinstance(node, ast.If):
            chain_nodes = list(ifs.chain(node))
            condition_count = sum(1 for n in chain_nodes[:-1] if isinstance(n, ast.If))
            
            # For multi-condition chains, apply special logic
            if condition_count > 1:
                # Check if this looks like the first node in the chain
                # (has elif branches) or if it's a continuation node
                is_first_in_chain = len(node.orelse) == 1 and isinstance(node.orelse[0], ast.If)
                
                if is_first_in_chain:
                    # This is definitely the first node, analyze the full chain
                    negated_count = 0
                    for chain_node in chain_nodes[:-1]:  # Exclude the final else body
                        if isinstance(chain_node, ast.If):
                            if (isinstance(chain_node.test, ast.UnaryOp) and isinstance(chain_node.test.op, ast.Not) or
                                isinstance(chain_node.test, ast.Compare) and any(isinstance(elem, ast.NotEq) for elem in chain_node.test.ops)):
                                negated_count += 1
                    
                    # Suppress violations if multiple conditions are negated
                    if negated_count > 1:
                        return
                else:
                    # This is likely a continuation node. To avoid duplicate violations,
                    # check if the parent chain was already analyzed and should be suppressed.
                    # For now, we'll suppress all violations in multi-condition chains
                    # to avoid the duplicate issue reported.
                    return

        # Normal checking  
        if isinstance(node.test, ast.UnaryOp):
            if isinstance(node.test.op, ast.Not):
                self.add_violation(refactoring.NegatedConditionsViolation(node))
        elif isinstance(node.test, ast.Compare) and any(

        # Special handling for multi-condition chains to prevent awkward rewriting
        if isinstance(node, ast.If):

        if isinstance(node, ast.If) and not ifs.has_else(node):
            return

        # Check if this looks like the start of a multi-condition chain
        if isinstance(node, ast.If) and len(node.orelse) == 1 and isinstance(node.orelse[0], ast.If):
            # This is the first node in a multi-condition chain, analyze the full chain
            chain_nodes = list(ifs.chain(node))
            condition_count = sum(1 for n in chain_nodes[:-1] if isinstance(n, ast.If))
            
            if condition_count > 1:
                # Count how many conditions in the full chain are negated
                negated_count = 0
                for chain_node in chain_nodes[:-1]:  # Exclude the final else body
                    if isinstance(chain_node, ast.If):
                        if (isinstance(chain_node.test, ast.UnaryOp) and isinstance(chain_node.test.op, ast.Not) or
                            isinstance(chain_node.test, ast.Compare) and any(isinstance(elem, ast.NotEq) for elem in chain_node.test.ops)):

                # This prevents awkward rewriting and fixes the reported issue
                if negated_count >= 1:

        if isinstance(node.test, ast.UnaryOp):
            if isinstance(node.test.op, ast.Not):
                self.add_violation(refactoring.NegatedConditionsViolation(node))

2. ❌ **Simple negated with else**: Now suppressed (but should trigger violation)

The problem is that my continuation detection is too broad. It's catching single if-else statements that aren't actually part of multi-condition chains.

The debug shows "DEBUG: Suppressing violation for continuation node" for the simple case, which means my heuristic is incorrectly identifying it as a continuation.

Let me refine the continuation detection logic. A single if-else statement should NOT be treated as a continuation.

The key difference is:
- Real continuations: Part of a larger chain analyzed by the first node
- Single if-else: Standalone statement that should still trigger violations

Let me make the continuation logic more restrictive:<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>str_replace</arg_value><arg_key>new_str</arg_key><arg_value>        elif isinstance(node, ast.If):
            # Check if this looks like a continuation node in a multi-condition chain
            # Heuristic: if this has an else clause but doesn't look like it starts a chain,
            # AND if the condition is negated, it might be a continuation that should be suppressed
            chain_nodes = list(ifs.chain(node))
            condition_count = sum(1 for n in chain_nodes[:-1] if isinstance(n, ast.If))
            
            # Only consider suppressing if this looks like it might be part of a larger pattern
            # that was already handled by the first branch
            if (condition_count == 1 and ifs.has_else(node) and
                # Additional restriction: only suppress negated conditions
                (isinstance(node.test, ast.UnaryOp) and isinstance(node.test.op, ast.Not) or
                 isinstance(node.test, ast.Compare) and any(isinstance(elem, ast.NotEq) for elem in node.test.ops))):
                
                # More precise heuristic: suppress only if this really looks like a continuation
                # that would have been part of a multi-condition analysis
                looks_like_continuation = (
                    # Has else clause
                    ifs.has_else(node) and
                    # Doesn't look like it starts a chain (no elif branches)
                    not (len(node.orelse) == 1 and isinstance(node.orelse[0], ast.If))
                )
                
                if looks_like_continuation:
                    # As a safety measure, let's be very conservative and only suppress
                    # in cases where we're confident it's a duplicate issue
                    print(f"DEBUG: Suppressing violation for continuation node")

import ast
from wemake_python_styleguide.logic.tree.ifs import has_else

code = '''
if not some_condition:
    stmt1
else:
    stmt2
'''

print(f'has_else: {has_else(if_node)}')

print(f'condition_count: {condition_count}')
print(f'Matches continuation condition: {condition_count == 1 and has_else(if_node)}')

from wemake_python_styleguide.logic.tree.ifs import has_else
code = '''
if not some_condition:
    stmt1
else:
    stmt2
'''
tree = ast.parse(code)
if_node = tree.body[0]
print('=== Simple if-else properties ===')
print(f'orelse length: {len(if_node.orelse)}')
print(f'orelse[0] type: {type(if_node.orelse[0]).__name__ if if_node.orelse else None}')
print(f'Looks like chain start: {len(if_node.orelse) == 1 and isinstance(if_node.orelse[0], ast.If)}')
print(f'has_else: {has_else(if_node)}')

print(f'condition_count: {condition_count}')
print(f'Matches continuation condition: {condition_count == 1 and has_else(if_node)}')
